Figure 5
.
Build-Back of a Single Component: Equations

Definition 4.1 (Build-Back of a Single Component).

Given a set of participants
E
, we define the

build-back of a single component
as the partial recursive
function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
. The recursive equations are
given in Figure
5
; if none of those apply or if

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\nsubseteq E
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
is undefined.

The rest of this subsection is dedicated to discussing the intuition behind the function
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}
and its correctness (Theorem
4.4
). First, let us consider the following equations from Definition
4.1
:

\small\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}\}_{i\in I}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}\}_{i\in I}}\\
\end{array}

The two equations above show how our compatibility (Equation
C
)
comes into play when building back a more general type. E.g.,
when the projection of

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
onto
E
is equal to the localisation
of a send type
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(with
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\in E
) then
their build-back is

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
The case where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small S}}}_{i}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is analogous.

The next example shows
how the build-back
retains the information both

(1)

internal to the component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

(2)

about the inter-component communication of the system, given by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Example 4.2 (Build-Back, Intuition).

We are given
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
with internal participants
E=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}
,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
for compatibility, describing inter-component communication in the system:
compatibility
C
holds.
Following the equations in Figure
5
,
we first
build back
the prefix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
then recursively the prefix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and ultimately
we exploit compatibility and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
gets absorbed into

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Namely,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}.}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\allowbreak\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
We observe that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
contains
all the interactions
,
both intra-component (in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) and
inter-component (in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
); in other words,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
carries the information
both in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
This property of the build-back is formalised by conclusions
(1)
and
(2)

of Theorem
4.4
.

Some detail from the previous example is hidden in
the auxiliary “unmerge” functions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmP}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmL}}
,
for projection and localiser respectively.
They reproduce in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}

a branching structure that is faithful to the branching both in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, where such branching may have been
merged when projecting
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
on
E=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}

(with
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sqcup}
, see Definition
3.6
)
or when localising
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(with
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sqcup^{L}}
, see Definition
3.8
).
We present the unmerge mechanism
with the next example, while, for
formal details,
we refer the interested reader
to Appendix
B.1
.

Example 4.3 (Unmerge).

We focus on the merge for the localiser
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sqcup^{L}}
; the case
for projection is analogous.
We are given
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{11}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{12}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\allowbreak\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\allowbreak\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In this case,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
In particular, when computing
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
a merge of branches happens:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sqcup^{L}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
When building back, we need to unmerge and reproduce the original
branching from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. In particular

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}=(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sqcup^{L}}(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})

Under this hypothesis,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmL}}

returns
suitable branches for the build-back:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmL}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;[\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]=[\ \color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ ,\ \color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ ]

Then, by following the build-back algorithm we obtain:

\small{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{11}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{12}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We observe that, above,
the output of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmL}}
, with arguments
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{H^{\dagger}}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{22}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

[\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{L}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{L}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]=[\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]
,
is a list of two types

[\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]=\allowbreak[\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\allowbreak\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{21}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]
;
for these, in particular, the following properties hold:

(a)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{L}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, for
i=1,2

(b)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{H^{\dagger}}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sqcup^{L}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}
, for any
E^{\prime}
, set of participants such that
E\cap E^{\prime}=\emptyset
.

The nesting of branching for general
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

may be intricate and tedious; the auxiliary functions

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmP}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmL}}
take care of the detail
(see Appendix
B.1
),
in a way that properties
(a)
and
(b)
as above
hold for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmL}}
, and similar ones for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf unmP}}

(Lemmas
B.11
and
B.15
,
Appendix
B.1
, ).
Generally, such properties ensure that both conclusions
(1)
and
(2)

(essential for composing multiple
subprotocols), of Theorem
4.4
, hold.

Finally, we can state our first compositionality result,
which certifies the definition of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}^{1}}
.

Theorem 4.4 (Building Back a Single Component).

We fix a set of participants
E
, and we are given hybrid types
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, such that:

(a)

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\subseteq E
,

(b)

\textsf{epart}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\cap E=\emptyset
, and

(c)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, (compatibility
C
).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
and we have:

(1)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

(2)

for all
E^{\prime}
, such that
E^{\prime}\cap E=\emptyset
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}
.

Moreover if
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
then
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

The proof of Theorem
4.4
proceeds by induction on

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf{depth}}(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\sf{depth}}(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
.
Its inductive structure follows the
defining equations of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}^{1}}
(Figure
5
)
and it is non-trivial; the full detail
can be found in Appendix

B.1
(Theorem
B.17
),
together with the auxiliary lemmas for merging. Theorem
4.4

ensures that the result of building back (backwards,
with respect to the usual direction of projection)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}

contains both (1) the information for the internal communication
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(i.e.,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) and (2) the information for
the external communication prescribed by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(i.e., for all
E^{\prime}
,
such that
E^{\prime}\cap E=\emptyset
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}
).
We describe the algorithm of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}^{1}}
and the proof outline
of Theorem
4.4
below, via example.

Example 4.5 (Definition
4.1
and Theorem
4.4
).

From Example
3.5
, we consider the subprotocol for the strategy department.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{go}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

The following protocol, described by the chief designer of the company
D
, coordinates the communication among the three departments (and ignores their internal one).

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{price}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We observe that
{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}\}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

To obtain
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}\}}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
, we first build back the internal global prefix in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
:

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We then proceed by induction, namely
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is built by composing
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and the smaller hybrid type obtained from

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. We observe that the first two send constructs correspond to the projection of the two initial messages of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(this is guaranteed by the compatibility condition
C
); we take:

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}{}^{\prime\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

To obtain
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}{}^{\prime\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
we observe that the compatibility condition takes care of the recursive construct
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and of the first message
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. After that, in each branch, we need to add first the internal message in

and then the external messages given by
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. We obtain

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}{}^{\prime\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{go}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{price}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and, ultimately,

\begin{array}[]{ll}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=&\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{go}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{price}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\\
{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\end{array}\right\}}\end{array}

Indeed,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
contains all interactions from both
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. The recursive definition of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}^{1}}
(Definition
4.1
) and the inductive proof of Theorem
4.4
follow the procedure presented in this example (Theorem
B.17
, Appendix
B.1
).

Composing Multiple Protocols: Theorem

4.4
as the base case, Theorem

4.7
as the inductive step.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
composes the subprotocol
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

with the compatibility type
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Here, we iterate this process
for an arbitrary number of subprotocols
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
whenever
compatible with respect to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(Equation
C
):
we achieve full

multiparty compositionality
of subprotocols.
The overview is given in Figure
6
.

Definition 4.6 (Build-Back).

Given a list of (disjoint) sets of participants
L=[E_{1},\dots,E_{N}]
, we define the partial recursive function
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}_{L}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
as follows:

\small\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}_{[E_{1}]}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E_{1}}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}_{[E_{1},\dots,E_{n+1}]}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{n+1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}_{[E_{2},\dots,E_{n+1}]}\;({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E_{1}}}\;\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{n+1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}\end{array}

From now on, we leave implicit the first list argument (of sets of participants): we write

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}_{L}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
.

Theorem 4.7 (Compositionality for Multiple Protocols).

We are given
E_{1},\dots,E_{N}
sets of roles, and the hybrid types
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, such that:

(a)

E_{i}\cap E_{j}=\emptyset
for all
i\neq j
,

(b)

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\subseteq E_{i}
for all
i
,

(c)

\textsf{epart}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\cap E_{i}=\emptyset
for all
i
, and

(d)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(compatibility
C
).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
and we have that, for all
i
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Moreover if
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
then
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Proof.

By induction on
N
. We add to the thesis:
(\textit{dsj})
for all
E^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime}\cap\bigcup_{i=1,\dots,N}E_{i}=\emptyset
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}
, since we need
(\textit{dsj})
within the induction hypothesis.
The case
N=1
is Theorem
4.4
. For
N=n+1
, we set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{H^{\dagger}}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{E_{1}}}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}}
and we apply the induction hypothesis
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}_{[E_{2},\dots,E_{n+1}]}\;(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{H^{\dagger}}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{n+1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
: we obtain that for
i=2,\dots,n
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. For
E_{1}
, since
E_{1}\cap\bigcup_{i=2,\dots,n+1}E_{i}=\emptyset
, thanks to
(\textit{dsj})
, we have
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{1}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{H^{\dagger}}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{1}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(by Theorem
4.4
). For
E^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime}\cap\bigcup_{i=1,\dots,n+1}E_{i}=\emptyset
, we have that
E^{\prime}\cap\bigcup_{i=2,\dots,n+1}E_{i}=\emptyset
, and hence for
(\textit{dsj})
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}
. We conclude by observing that
E^{\prime}\cap E_{1}=\emptyset
and thus, by Theorem
4.4
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E^{\prime}}}
.
∎

Example 4.8 (Theorem
4.7
).

In Example
4.5
, we have seen how to build back
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
into
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, a new hybrid type containing the information both for the inter-protocol communication (from
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) and for the communication inside the strategy department (from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
). We observe that compatibility
C
holds not only for
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, but also for
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf sales}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, namely:

\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf sales}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{w}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{price}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}},{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}2}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}?{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{price}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\\
\end{array}

The hypothesis of Theorem
4.7
holds and thus we can build
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}\;(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf sales}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
, such that
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{w}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf sales}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}},{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}2}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. To make the construction of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
explicit, we follow the inductive proof structure of Theorem
4.7
. The base case is taken care of in Example
4.5
, where we apply Theorem
4.4
and build
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, by composition of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we obtain :

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\\

We observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{w}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{w}}}}\}}}
, hence, since

still holds, we can apply again the build-back procedure and obtain:

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\\

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
collects
all the interactions from
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

.

that also includes

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
we perform one more
induction step,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{\dagger}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}2}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\\

A graphical representation of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
can be found in Appendix
A
.

Theorem
4.7
gives
a technique for composing
multiple subprotocols into a more general one.
The next, conclusive step
proves
that compositionality
well-behaves with respect to
MPST projection.

4.3.
Step 3: Compositionality through Projection

With Definition
3.6
, we have generalised the MPST projection to hybrid types. We prove that generalised projection well-behaves with respect to set inclusion.

Theorem 4.9 (Projection Composes over Set Inclusion).

Given
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
,
E_{2}\subseteq E_{1}
, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{1}}}
is defined, then
(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{1}}}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{2}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{2}}}\
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(see Appendix
B.2
).
∎

Theorem
4.9
is the last fundamental
ingredient to achieve

distributed protocol specification
.

Corollary 4.10 (Distributed Protocol Specification).

Given
E_{1},\dots,E_{N}
disjoint sets of participants, a global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
hybrid types, such that:

(a)

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})=E_{i}
for all
i
,

(b)

\textsf{epart}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\cap E_{i}=\emptyset
for all
i
,

(c)

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1,\dots,N}E_{i}
, and

(d)

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(compatibility
C
);

there exists
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that, for all
i
, for all
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\in E_{i}
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
.

Proof.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{B}\;(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\;([\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}])}}
; by Theorem
4.7
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is global and such that
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Then, we apply Theorem
4.9
and we obtain, for
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\in E_{i}
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
. We observe that
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
is a local type, for all
r
, (Remark
3.7
).
∎

Example 4.11.

In Examples
4.5
and
4.8
,
the protocol designer for each department
has given their hybrid type,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf sales}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
disciplining internal communication (with messages
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
)
and specifying the communication with
other departments
(with sends/receives,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
/
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
).
Compatibility (Equation
C
)
has been verified against
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, as described by the chief designer
D
.
In (Example
4.8
) we build back

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(we observe that is
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
implies
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
).
Corollary
4.10
holds and projections of
components
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf sales}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

onto single participants are local types,
also projections of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(Theorem
4.9
),
the global type disciplining
the whole system.
For instance, if we
wanted to get the local type for
d
, traditionally,
we would do so by projecting

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. With our distributed protocol specification, it
is enough to project
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
onto
d
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\}}}=(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}\}}}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf str}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}\}}}=\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 18.49988pt\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{prod}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small nat}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{go}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{d}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ad}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{wait}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\end{array}

If instead want to implement processes for
{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}2}}
,
we can obtain the local types from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf fin}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{f}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}1}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf
