We can proceed analogously
for all participants.

Remark 4.12 (Applicability and Preservation of Semantics).

Example
4.11
displays the essence of
of our theory,
formally captured by Corollary
4.10
:
for a system specified in a distributed way,
with components
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
compatibility type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
there is

no need for an explicit description of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. After compatibility checks (Equation
C
),
our theory builds back a well-formed global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for the whole system, and
the session of local types,
projections of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, can be obtained,
in a distributed fashion, by directly
projecting subprotocols,
since, for
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\in{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\}}}
.

At the design stage,
each designer
D_{i}

gives the subprotocol
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and,
with the simple equality check
C
,
they make sure that their protocol is compatible
with
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(described by the chief designer
D
).
At the type-checking/implementation stage,
the designer
D_{i}

independently

obtains local types for
well-behaved implementations
directly from their specification
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

D_{i}
is never concerned
with the communication happening
internally to, or among, other components.
What guarantees global well-behaviour
is the existence of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, proved
once and for all by our theory;
no designer or programmer needs
its explicit description.
We have achieved

distributed protocol specification
.

Desirable MPST semantic guarantees,
such as liveness and deadlock freedom,
are preserved by our theory, thanks
to its
semantics preservation
. Our compositionality-through-projection
technique can explicitly build back
a protocol as a global type that is
traditionally well-formed
(see, e.g.,
(
Honda et al. 2016
;
Deniélou and Yoshida 2013
)
).
Thus, our theory brings modularity to
the protocol design phase,
but, after such distributed specification,
the result is a
traditional MPST system,
with a
single
global type that
projects on local types for

all
participants,
which benefits from existing
semantics results
from the MPST literature.

Remark 4.13 (On Hybrid Types).

Central to this work
is Definition
3.3

of hybrid types.
Our theory
shows how
“open” subprotocols, interacting
with other components of the system,
can be specified as
hybrid types
,
safely composed into a
global type
,
and projected onto
local types
.

The syntax of hybrid types is
simply the combination of the
syntaxes of global and local types
and, through the predicates

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{isGlobal(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{isLocal(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,1})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
we can isolate
global and local types
respectively,
from the rest of hybrid types.
This choice
makes our development compatible
with existing MPST systems and
is key to semantics preservation:
in our compositionality
theory, well-formed global types
guarantee semantics properties
and local types are used for
participant implementation,
exactly as in traditional MPST
(see Corollary
4.10

and Remark
4.12
).

At the same time, dealing with
a single syntax (hybrid types)
simplifies our theory
significantly. This paper proposes
an approach to protocol compositionality
that heavily relies on projection.
Traditional MPST projection
operates on global types and returns
local types for implementation.
Our generalised projection, instead,
takes a hybrid type and returns a hybrid type,
but, since global and local types
are hybrid types, our projection
maintains and extends
the functionalities of the traditional
operator. The main gain is flexibility:
a function with the same domain as its
codomain can be composed with itself
and this is central in our proofs
(see Theorem
4.9

and its role in the proof of Corollary
4.10
).
In other words, instead of working with multiple
operators (which would have very similar definitions)
and proving them compatible, we
rely on a single one: generalised projection.
In particular, we apply projection
in the following key steps of our development:

•

projecting the component
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
of a system
onto one of its internal participants
p
, gives—as
it is customary in the literature—the local type

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{L}{}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for implementing
p
;

•

projecting the type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is necessary for compatibility
C
:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
; and

•

the preservation of projection (Theorem
4.9
)
ensures
applicability and correctness:
local types for implementation, obtained
by separately projecting the distributed components, are
also projections
of a single, well-formed global type for the whole system
(equation
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
,
Corollary
4.10
).

5.
Case Studies

In this section, we evaluate our development
with case studies. In §
5.1
, we discuss the role
of the
parallel construct

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}|\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

in our protocol composition.
Then, we consider the
industry-standard protocol for authorisation,
OAuth 2.0,
(
Hardt 2012
;
Horne 2020
)
:
in §
5.2
, we observe the
modularity

benefits of our theory, and, in §
5.3
,
we reach an
optimisation
for it.
In §
5.4
, we show
how hybrid types
can be smoothly extended to feature

delegation and explicit connections
.

5.1.
On the Parallel Construct and Compositionality

Our work enriches the type system
of MPST with compositionality
at the protocol-description level,
while retaining the traditional syntax
of local types. Consequently,
the targeted process language is
standard
(
Castro-Perez et al. 2021
;
Glabbeek et al. 2021
)
.
In particular, local types and
processes are
single-threaded
.
On the other hand,
to allow the description of
protocols where two different components
execute independently, without
exchanging messages with each other,
we have added the
parallel construct
to the syntax of hybrid
(and global) types.

The

parallel construct
(or
parallel composition
)
for global types
appears in
the first presentation of MPST

(
Honda et al. 2008
)
,
but dismissed in subsequent literature

(
Bettini et al. 2008
;
Coppo et al. 2015
;
Castro-Perez et al. 2021
)
.
For achieving compositionality,
without requiring further well-formedness restrictions on global types, we need to explicitly add parallel composition

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}|\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Let us consider the following distributed specification:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hskip 18.49988pt\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{1}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hskip 18.49988pt\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{2}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{Y}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{3}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{Y}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{4}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Compatibility holds:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\allowbreak={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{1}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\}}}\allowbreak=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{1}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Our theory guarantees the existence of a well-formed global type
for the whole system, which entails a deadlock-free session:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}.}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\ \big(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{2}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\big)\ |\ \big(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{Y}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{3}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{Y}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{4}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\big)\ }\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Without parallel composition,
it is not clear how
to compose

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{1}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{2}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
even if they are compatible
with respect to

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

1
1

1

For more details on the role of parallel composition in the inductive proof of Theorem
4.4
, see the full proof in Appendix
B.1
, Theorem
B.17
Indeed, recent work
(
Glabbeek et al. 2021
)
has
drawn attention to the role of the parallel
construct in traditional MPST: by exploiting
a similar example to the
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{\sf par}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
above, the authors
show that, if the syntax does
not include parallel composition,
there are non-deadlocked sessions
that do not have a global type.

5.2.
Distributed Specification for OAuth 2.0

We consider the industry-standard protocol for authorisation, OAuth 2.0,
(
Hardt 2012
;
Horne 2020
)
. In such protocol, the owner of a resource gives approval (through the OAuth server) for an external application to access some resource; the OAuth server ensures, by means of tokens, that the sensitive data of the owner are not shared with the external application.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf auth}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{init}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small id}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small scp}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ow}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{login}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small id}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small scp}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ow}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}:}\\

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{close}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{release}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{code}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small code}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{exchange}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small id}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small secret}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small code}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{close}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{release}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{token}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small token}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{pass}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\}}\end{array}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\}}\end{array}

Figure 7
.
Distributed Specification for OAuth 2.0

We present a specification for the OAuth 2.0 protocol in two components (Figure
7
): a first designer
D_{\sf auth}
gives the hybrid type
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf auth}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for the
OAuth server

oa
and the resource
owner

ow
, while a second
D_{\sf res}
is in charge of the interactions involving an
untrusted app

ua
and the
resource service

res
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Separately,
a chief designer
describes the compatibility type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Compatibility holds,

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ow}}}}\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf auth}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and Corollary
4.10
guarantees the existence of a well-formed global type for the whole protocol.

Let us focus on the component
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
and in particular on its subcomponent
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{{\sf res\_acc}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, which contains exclusively internal interactions:
once authorisation is granted, the
untrusted app communicates
directly with the resource service.
We observe then that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{{\sf res\_acc}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

can be specified
modularly
:
the designer
D_{\sf res}
could, at a later stage,

specify a different protocol
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}^{\prime}_{{\sf res\_acc}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
for the interaction between
ua
and
res
,

without affecting compatibility
. We recognise one extra benefit of our theory
in
modular specification for intra-component communication
:
a designer (
D_{\sf res}
) can modify the specification
of their protocol (
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) over time,
in its internal interactions (
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{{\sf res\_acc}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}^{\prime}_{{\sf res\_acc}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
),
as long as its external communication (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
)—and hence compatibility
with respect to the prescription of the chief designer
(
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
)—is preserved.

5.3.
Optimisation of OAuth 2.0 Specification

By inspecting the types in Figure
7
,
we notice that all inter-component interactions (between
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf auth}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) go through the participant
oa

and, hence, they are all
documented by
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, but also in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf auth}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Here, describing explicitly
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
looks redundant and we ask ourselves whether a more
efficient specification is possible.
It turns out
that we can omit the specification of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf oa}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for
the OAuth 2.0 protocol and

optimise distributed specification
(Corollary
4.10
)
in general.

Corollary 5.1 (Distributed MPST Specification, Optimisation).

Given
E,E_{1},\dots,E_{N}
disjoint sets of participants, a global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
hybrid types, such that:

(a)

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\allowbreak=E_{i}
, for all
i=1,\dots,N
,

(b)

\textsf{epart}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\cap E_{i}\allowbreak=\emptyset
, for all
i
,

(c)

{\textsf{ipart}}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\allowbreak\subseteq\allowbreak(\bigcup_{i=1,\dots,N}E_{i})\cup E
, and

(d)

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
;

there exists
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that, for all
i=1,\dots,N
, for all
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\in E_{i}
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
, and for all
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\in E
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}}}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf op}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{init}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small id}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small scp}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ow}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{login}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small id}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small scp}}}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ow}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}:}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{deny}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{close}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{release}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\\
{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{auth}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small name}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small pwd}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}};}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{close}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{release}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{code}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small code}}}).}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{exchange}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small id}}},{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small secret}}},\texttt{\small)}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{close}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{release}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{token}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\texttt{\small token}}}).\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}:}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\{{\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{pass}}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\}}\end{array}\right\}}\end{array}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\}\}}\\
\text{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$ (and $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{{\sf res\_acc}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$) the same as in Figure \ref{fig:oauth}}\end{array}

Figure 8
.
Distributed for OAuth 2.0, Optimised

The proof of the above corollary is analogous to the proof of Corollary
4.10
and entails the same semantic guarantees, since it leads to the existence of a global type for the whole system. However, in Corollary
5.1
,
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
plays a twofold role:

(a)

it is the hybrid type, communication subprotocol for the component
with set of participants
E
, and

(b)

it is the compatibility protocol, carrying all the inter-component
interactions of the system.

For OAuth 2.0,
by exploiting Corollary
5.1
,
we obtain a more efficient distributed
specification (Figure
8
).
Only two hybrid types, one for each component,
are specified, provided that
one contains also the compatibility information.
We observe, with notations from Figures
7

and
8
that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf auth}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf op}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\}}}
, for
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\in\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{oa}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ow}}}}\}
. Hence, for all participants,
in both specifications we obtain the same local
types for implementation. However, in the optimised case of
Figure
8
, for compatibility, we only need to check one equality:
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}_{\sf op}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{ua}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{res}}}}\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{\sf res}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Remark 5.2 (Local Types from Partial Protocols).

We observe that Corollaries
4.10

and
5.1
prescribe differently
how to obtain local types for implementation.
In Corollary
4.10
, we
project each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
onto its internal participants (in
E_{i}
)
to get the right local types.
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
has the sole role of disciplining
inter-component communication for compatibility,
hence all its projections onto single
participants (even if well-defined) are
not
meant
for implementation. In Corollary
5.1
,
instead, the global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
not
only provides for compatibility, but also
describes the internal communication
of the component that is concerned
with the participants in
E
.
Thus, if
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\in E_{i}
, the respective
local type is obtained by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
,
while, if
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\in E
, the local type for implementing

r
is
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}}}
.

5.4.
An Extension to Delegation and Explicit Connections

The MPST literature offers a
variety of formalisms that enrich
the type system with expressive
features

(
Viering et al. 2021
;
Bocchi et al. 2014
;
Zhou et al. 2020
;
Castro-Perez and Yoshida 2020
;
Lagaillardie et al. 2022
)
, while
maintaining the central mechanism
of projecting global types onto
local types for distributed
implementation. This
suggests that our
compositional methodology
is general enough to capture
more sophisticated formalisms
than core MPST.
In this section,
we support this intuition
with a case study:
we extend
hybrid types to include

delegation

and
explicit connections
.
At the end of the section
(Example
5.7
),
we show that this suitability
for extensions is a
prerogative of
our compositionality-through-projection,
differently from
other compositionality approaches
based on input/output matching

(
Barbanera et al. 2021
;
Stolze et al. 2021
)
.

Both delegation and explicit connections
are relevant and practical features,
which have been extensively studied by
the literature on concurrency.
Delegation—the mechanism
in which a participant appoints a different
participant to act on their behalf—first
appears in the context
of object-oriented concurrency

(
Ungar and Smith 1987
;
Yonezawa and Tokoro 1986
;
Agha 1990
)

and, naturally, it has been implemented in
mainstream object-oriented languages

(
Imai et al. 2020
;
Hu et al. 2008
;
Scalas and Yoshida 2016
)
.
Over the years,
the session-type literature has investigated the
verification of concurrency
in the presence of delegation

(
Honda et al. 1998
;
Honda et al. 2008
;
Bettini et al. 2008
;
Scalas et al. 2017
)
.
In particular, we take the approach
of
Castellani et al. 2020
,
who treat delegation as internal
to the session, in contrast with
channel-passing delegation,
which requires the interleaving of sessions.
Thus, the authors can model (internal)
delegation simply by adding specific
constructs to the syntax of global types.
Moreover, global types from this paper
benefit from the flexibility of
explicit connections:
some participant may or may not
take part in the communication,
depending on the
choice made by some other participant
at a previous stage of execution.
Explicit connections are common
in the design of real-world protocols

(
Hardt 2012
;
MIT 2022
)
and
have been significantly addressed by
the literature

(
Hu and Yoshida 2017
;
Castellani et al. 2019
;
Harvey et al. 2021
;
Gheri et al. 2022
)
.
Specifically, our
approach is immediately
compatible with
the type system
and the semantics in

(
Castellani et al. 2020
)

(from which we take
most notation):
we can compose subprotocols into
a well-formed and

well-delegated
global type,
projecting on
local session types
for the whole communicating system.
Thus, no new semantics is needed,
but MPST semantics guarantees
(subject reduction, session fidelity, and progress)
hold.

For this case study, we focus on
our novel notion of
compatibility through projection
(Equation
C
).
Developing the full theory
goes beyond the scope of this paper:
the structure of the proofs
would be exactly the same as
in our core theory
(Section
4
).
Instead, we

(a)

extend hybrid types to delegation and explicit connections,

(b)

generalise projection,

(c)

define the localiser, and

(d)

state compatibility.

Definition 5.3 (Hybrid Types with Delegation).

We define a set of
prefixes
for global and local messages;
when
p
establishes an
explicit connection
to
q
,
we use the superscript
e
.

\begin{array}[]{llll}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}::=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}&\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{out}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}::=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}&\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{in}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}::=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}&\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}::=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{out}}}\end{array}

Hybrid types with delegation
are defined inductively by:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}::=&\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{1}|\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bigwedge_{i\in I}\pi^{\textsf{in}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}\\
&\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~~\mathbf{|\!\!|}~~\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\end{array}

Without loss of generality, we omit payload types
(sorts) from the syntax above: formally,
participants only exchange labels
{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}
.
For degenerate branchings (with a single branch, where no actual choice happens)
we omit the operators
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bigwedge}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and we simply write the message (starting with a prefix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{out}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{in}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
).

Hybrid types with delegation (Definition
5.3
)
endow with local send/receive constructs
the global types from
(
Castellani et al. 2020
)
.
In particular,
global choices

and
union types
from
(
Castellani et al. 2020
)

are particular cases of the above syntax.
The global choice,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\alpha}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, is obtained
by asking that, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
are global messages,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\alpha}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Local union (send) types,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bigvee_{i\in I}\pi^{\textsf{out}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, are now written as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
where all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
are send constructs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{out}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
In what follows we discuss how Definition
5.3

is a direct extension of Definition
3.3
.

We observe that branching is more permissive than in
Definition
3.3
.

•

In a single choice
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
the sender
p
is unique, but
receivers may be different, internal or external:
in choices we allow the mixing of global messages
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and send constructs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{out}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

•

Intersection types
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bigwedge_{i\in I}\pi^{\textsf{in}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
combine receive constructs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{in}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, possibly
with different senders (and receivers).

Remark 5.4 (Generalising Hybrid Types to Delegation).

The syntax of hybrid types with delegation
(Definition
5.3
) is a generalisation
of the core syntax
of hybrid types (Definition
3.3
).

2
2

2

In order to make the notation lighter,
we ignore sorts in both syntaxes:
only labels
{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}}
are sent.

For each construct in
Definition
3.3
,
we show how it can be expressed in
the formalism of
5.3
.

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{X}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}|\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

are preserved.

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

can be written as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, with

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for all
i\in I
.

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

can be written as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bigwedge_{i\in I}\pi^{\textsf{in}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, with

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\pi^{\textsf{in}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for all
i\in I
.

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}\to{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}};\{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}}.\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

can be written as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus_{i\in I}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{i}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, with

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\sigma}{}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for all
i\in I
.

The global construct
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
models a
forward delegation

where
p
delegates their behaviour to
q
; then,
such behaviour is given back with the global construct
for
backward delegation
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
The notation for local constructs is analogous:

active forward delegation

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,

passive backward delegation

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,

passive forward delegation

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and

active backward delegation

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
As in our core theory
(see Definition
3.3
),
local constructs carry
both internal participants
and external ones (in square brackets):
e.g., in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,

p
(internal) delegates their behaviour to
the participant
q
of a different component (external).

Example 5.5 (Global Types with Delegation and Explicit Connections).

Here, we consider two simple examples
that display in isolation the features
of delegation and explicit connections.
Let us consider the following global protocols,
written with the syntax of
hybrid types with delegation (Definition
5.3
).

\small\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{card}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
after the
customer
has given the
ok
,
the
seller

delegates
to the
bank
their role
in the communication
(
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}
).
Namely, when the
customer
sends their
card
number to
the
seller
, they are in fact sharing that information
with the
bank
. With the construct
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}
,
the
bank
delegates back their role to the
seller
.

\small\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{location}}}}});\boxplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{open}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{EUfrontpage}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{open}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{UKfrontpage}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\end{array}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

In
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, after the
website
receives
the
user
’s location, it decides whether
to
(explicitly) connect
them to the
EUshop
or to the

UKshop
. Then the
EUshop

(resp. the
UKshop
)
sends the
EUfrontpage

(resp. the
UKfrontpage
)
to the
user
. Here in the first
branch (resp. the second) of the choice,
there is an explicit connection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) to
the participant

EUshop
(resp.
UKshop
),
which does not appear in the other branch.

We adopt the definition of
well-delegated

type from
(
Castellani et al. 2020
)

(Definition
4.11
); in particular
a forward delegation (e.g.,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
)
is always followed by a corresponding
backwards one (e.g.,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
);
also, choices must not appear between
corresponding forward and backward delegation.

We define
projection
and
localiser

for hybrid types with delegation, by extending
Definitions
3.6
and
3.8
,
§
3.2
.
The main differences are listed below.

•

Projection behaves on
delegation constructs

following the same intuition as for messages. E.g.,

(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

if
\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\}\subseteq E
, or

(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}];(\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright^{d}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{({{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}})\},E}})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

if
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\in E
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\notin E
,
where projection needs to keep track of
p
delegating to
q
,
as indicated by the notation

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright^{d}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{({{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}})\},E}}
.

•

Branches with explicit connections of a participant
s
can be merged with branches where
s
does not appear; e.g., if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}=\boxplus\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\allowbreak\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

•

We allow merging of receive constructs with different senders; e.g., with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
as in the
bullet point above,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bigwedge\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

•

As in our core theory §
3.2
, the localiser skips global constructs and retains local ones; e.g.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}];({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1})}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

The partial function

\_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright^{d}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathit{dE},E}}

generalises the delegation projection functions

\_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright^{1}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{({{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}})}}
and
\_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright^{2}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{({{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}})}}
from
(
Castellani et al. 2020
)

(Figure
6
); this function
is of a sequential nature: it is not defined
for branching, recursion, and parallel constructs.
Full definitions of projection
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}
,
the auxiliary delegation projection
\upharpoonright^{d}
,
and localiser
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}{}\!\!
are in Appendix
B.3
and compatible with
(
Castellani et al. 2020
)
.

Example 5.6 (Projection with Delegation and Explicit Connections).

Let us consider
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
from Example
5.5

and, in particular, their projections onto single participants

The intuition behind the delegation constructs in
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

is well displayed by its projections.

\small\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{ok}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{card}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}};\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{customer}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{card}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}];\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\end{array}

After
actively

delegating their role to the
bank

(
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}]}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
)
and before
passively

being delegated their role back
(
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
),
the
seller
is not involved
in any communication (
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{d}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}}}
).
At the same time, the
bank

plays the opposite role: is passive
in receiving the delegation from

seller
(
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}]\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\circ\langle\!\langle\bullet}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
)
and active in delegating the role back
(
{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{bank}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\bullet\rangle\!\rangle\circ}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{seller}}}}]
).
Furthermore, we observe that,
while the
bank

knows that they are receiving
the
card
number from the
customer
,
the
customer
acts as if they are
sending it directly to the

seller
:
as expected, the
customer

is not involved in
the delegation process
and hence ignores it.

When projecting
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
onto its participants,
we obtain the following types.

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{location}}}}});\bigwedge\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{EUfrontpage}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{UKfrontpage}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\end{array}\right\}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{location}}}}});\boxplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}!^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{open}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}!^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{open}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\end{array}\right\}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{open}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{EUfrontpage}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}{}_{ec}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}\}}}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{open}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{user}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({\textit{{{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{UKfrontpage}}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}}\end{array}

We observe that
the participants
EUshop

and
UKshop
are concerned
only with the interactions
that happen after their
explicit connection
(
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{EUshop}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{website}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{UKshop}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
respectively)
and not with the communication
in the other branch, where
they are not connected.

We now define
compatibility
.
In the usual multi-component scenario,
we give hybrid types
(with delegation and explicit connections)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, for each component,
and a compatibility type

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. As in §
4
,
we consider the generic
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\in\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{N}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}

(with
E
being the set of its internal participants)
in isolation
and we state compatibility:

(
D
)

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H^{E}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Equation
D
is exactly the same
as Equation
C
. This captures the
generality
of our technique:
given an existing top-down MPST system, first, we extend its syntax to hybrid types and
generalise projection to sets of participants;
then we isolate the inter-component communication of each protocol
with the localiser; and, finally, we can state compatibility,
prove compositionality, and achieve distributed protocol
specification.
Such general design gives a clear advantage to our theory, with respect
to the dual approach from previous work
(
Barbanera et al. 2021
;
Stolze et al. 2021
)
.

Example 5.7 (Compatibility Through Projection VS Input/Output Matching).

We consider the following three-component system
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
with
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
for compatibility.

\hskip-2.84526pt\begin{array}[]{ll}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}&\hskip-2.84526pt\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{1}}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}});\bigwedge\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}?{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\\
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\boxplus}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{0}}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}!^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{0}}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}&\hskip-2.84526pt\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}}?^{e}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}_{0}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}!{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\end{array}

In
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
p
makes a choice: on the first branch, first it sends
an internal message and then an external one to
q
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
;
on the other branch
p
first makes an explicit connection to
s

in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and then sends an internal message.
The component

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(after an internal interaction) is waiting to
receive either from
p
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
or from
s
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
The third component
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is concerned only with
the second branch, in case it is chosen by
p
.
All types
are compatible with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\upharpoonright}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{E_{i}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\sf{loc}}\;}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, with
E_{1}=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{0}}$}}}}\}
,
E_{2}=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{1}}$}}}}\}
,
and
E_{3}=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}\}
. Indeed, we can build a well-formed
global type for the whole system:

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{1}}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{1}}}});\boxplus\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\left\{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{0}}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1},\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to^{e}{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{r}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{e}_{0}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{s}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{q}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell^{\prime}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}\textbf{{p$\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{}_{0}}$}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5508}{\ell_{0}}}});\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\right\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We focus on
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and we observe that,
in the intersection of inputs,
q

is waiting to receive from
p
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
on the first branch, and from
s
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
on the second.
Therefore, a match for inputs in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{2}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
cannot
happen solely with outputs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, nor
solely with outputs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{H_{3}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
In this case, dual compatibility relations
would fail, while, with our approach based
on projection, compatibility
can be simply stated with respect to the
global guidance of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G^{\dagger}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, through
Equation
D
.

6.
Related Work

Our work achieves
protocol compositionality

in MPST top-down systems, namely those
systems
where the communication protocol
is explicitly described
as a global type
and, subsequently, from the projection of it,
local types are obtained for implementation.
We organise this section as a progressive discussion
of related work, with respect to our paper,
from more distant to closer.

MPST Alternatives to the Top-Down Approach.

Since their first appearance
(
Honda et al. 2008
)
,
MPST have evolved into a variety of
frameworks
for the specification and
the verification of concurrency,
often beyond the original
top-down approach.
The works of

Lange and Tuosto 2012

and
Deniélou and Yoshida 2013

explicitly give algorithms
for the synthesis
of global types from communicating finite-state machines, while

Lange et al. 2015
propose
a similar method to build
graphical choreographies,
expressed as global graphs.

Scalas and Yoshida 2019
develop a framework
where global types are not necessary,
relying instead on
model- and type-checking techniques
for verifying
safety properties of
collections of local types.
The advantage of such approaches is that they offer analysis
for pre-existent systems.
However, before the need to
hierarchically design
a new communicating system,
the top-down approach
enables a
high-level specification of the system
that guarantees safe interactions
of distributed implementations.
The top-down approach
has seen a variety of tools and implementations, e.g.,

(
Honda et al. 2011
;
Yoshida et al. 2013
;
Neykova and Yoshida 2019
;
Yoshida et al. 2021
;
Gheri et al. 2022
;
Castro-Perez et al. 2021
;
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2021
;
Carbone and Montesi 2013
)

and it has been investigated beyond MPST

(
Barbanera et al. 2020a
;
Montesi 2013
)
.
Recent research
(e.g.,
(
Glabbeek et al. 2021
;
Lagaillardie et al. 2022
;
Cledou et al. 2022
;
Jacobs et al. 2022
)
)
has kept exploring the
possibilities offered
by an explicit design of systems
through protocol specification.
Our work adds
compositionality
to top-down protocol
specification.

Protocol Flexibility and Modularity beyond MPST.

Caires and Vieira 2009
;
Padovani et al. 2014

do not
address protocol compositionality,
but, in defining conversation types,
the authors combine global and local constructs
in the same syntax, for
a flexible specification: messages can be scheduled,
while participants can be at first
left unspecified, thus
allowing for interleaving of sessions.
In the context of reactive programming,

Carbone et al. 2018
;
Savanovic et al. 2020

propose a technique for modular design:
